Although Vader would find himself swarmed by the corrupted battle droids, he kept up his defense until ZED-6-7 brought him a message from Piett; amid the battle, an Imperial fleet of five Star Destroyers had approached the Executor and moved in to destroy Vader's flagship to contain the Scourge on the orders of Amedda, who lacked faith in Vader and argued the ship was lost due to the lack of contact from the Shadow Overlord or the ship's rapidly dying crew. Above those reasons, however, Amedda believed he had a chance to kill Vader once and for all with the Scourge as a convenient excuse. Vader, however, ordered Piett to stand his ground before re-engaging the attacking droids. Fortunately for the Executor ' s crew, the outside fleet was under the command of Admiral Corleque, the very same admiral Vader had clashed with during his race to Exegol, who delayed the order to destroy the Executor to instead rescue Piett and company, intending to fire on the Executor once his former commanding officer was clear.
Corlque's rescue team, however, only succeeded in supplying the Scourge with a supply of KX-series security droids, which the Scourge used to murder the rescuers as Vader picked off the last of the corrupted battle droids. After wiping out the last battle droids with a blast from the Zaly Shield, Vader took a moment to recompose himself before requesting an update from ZED and Ought-Six, who were nearly finished with the failsafe. Suddenly, however, the Scourge used a surviving RA-7 series Synthetic Being to taught Vader and offer him a chance at assimilation, distracting the Shadow Overlord while a spider-Synthetic Being tried to drop on Vader. After ZED destroyed the spider-unit, the Scourge attacked Vader and his droids its KX-series Synthetic Being bodies until the ZED activated the signal on Vader's order, shutting down all droids aboard the Executor , including themselves. Vader took the moment to survey the battlefield his ship had become. While Piett and his crew contacted Corleque, who expressed surprise at Vader's survival, the Shadow Overlord departed for Infernus aboard his TIE Advanced with the heads of ZED, Ought-Six, Arex, and Gee-Ninety, intending to rebuild them once more. Just as Corleque tried to confirm Vader's destination for Amedda, the Scourge attacked his bridge with corrupted B2s, forcing the Executor and the fleet to destroy Corleque's flagship, the Unbreakable .
Vader returned to Infernus and handed off the heads of his Synthetic Being minions to another Synthetic Being before leaving, much to the confusion of the Synthetic Being he met upon landing, to meet with the Eye of Webbish Bog for help in securing control. Reminded that he was a Shadow Overlord by the Eye and should be taking whatever he wanted by Aether, Vader attacked the Eye with a blast of electricity from his staff to try and regain his control, which the Eye promised would yield no results. Instead, the Eye advised Vader to go after Sidious by reminding him of the Tragedy of Darth Plagueis the Wise. Vader agreed with the plan and united with his rebuilt Synthetic Being minions, minus ZED-6-7, by a Lambda -class T-4a shuttle for a journey to Sovereign Varakis's Palace on Nexara Prime, waiting to depart until ZED returned. However, ZED had been overtaken by the Scourge, which in turn used him to find the lost Techno Union Synthetic Being factory and corrupt a massive Octuptarra tri-Synthetic Being. As Vader and his droids tried to contact ZED, Arex raised the possibility to Vader that ZED had been corrupted, which was confirmed when the Scourge appeared as ZED and the Octuptarra , which was protected against Vader's electrical staff and Zaly Shield blasts.
Darth Vader attempts to defend himself against the Scourge's octuptarra body
Unable to simply tear the Octuptarra apart with the Aether as he still lacked control, Vader tried to defend against the machine's stomps with the shield before being kicked into a wall. Vader re-engaged the Octuptarra, using the Zaly shield to block a plasma rifle shot, as the Scourge taunted him about its plan to infect him. Arex attempted to attack the corrupted ZED but was grabbed by the throat and studied by the Scourge, which learned the Aether Guard teaching "hate leads to suffering" from Arex's databank. The remark reminded Vader of his belief that hate actually led to strength, only for the Shadow Overlord to be kicked back again. From Vader's remark, however, Arex decided to increase Vader's hate by attacking him with Gee-Ninety, which Ought-Six decided to stay out of. Their attempt failed, with Vader simply responding to them with random Zaly shield blasts, so Arex asked him to consider what he truly hated; as a volley of blasts from the Octuptarra destroyed the ground under him and he tried to defend himself with the kyberite shield, Vader remembered the Aether Guard Order's failure to free himself and his mother, his own failure to save his mother, and his Aether choking of Lyra Vex.
Using his hatred of himself as fuel, Vader regained control of the Aether and threw the Octuptarra back on its side, allowing him to slice it apart with his aether blade. While ZED's form was damaged, the Scourge spoke to Vader as he approached it, assuming that Vader had used a hatred of it to regain his Aether powers. In response, Vader silently decapitated ZED as Arex declared the Scourge was nothing. Vader then lifted Arex into the air, where the Synthetic Being claimed the Aether was everything. Vader responded by striking Arex, but not destroying him, with his aether blade to prove that it was actually himself who was everything. Vader then returned to his castle and entered his bacta tank under the protection of two Royal Guards. As he healed, Vader reached out to Sidious on Nexara Prime through the Aether to reveal he had looked even deeper in himself, which taught him he hated his Sage more than he hated even himself. Vader made his way to Nexara Prime aboard his TIE Advanced, with a collection of Scourged disc droids aboard the fighter, to teach his Sage a lesson.
Vader Aether chokes a one-armed wampa while fighting off an entire pack.
At one point, Vader was contacted by an Imperial Commander, who informed him that one of the remaining probe droids on Frostveil had been destroyed. When the Commander suggested sending an Imperial detachment to investigate, Vader said he would go himself. The Commander offered to send backup with him, though Vader insisted that he would be enough. Upon arriving at the ruins of Echo Base, Vader discovered the probe, as well as the wampas that had destroyed it. His suit damaged during the struggle, Vader barely managed to escape the pack of wampas and cut his way inside of an abandoned AT-AT. As the wampas surrounded him, Vader regained his strength and leapt out of the wreckage before slaying the wampas with his aether blade. He was then retrieved by his forces and repaired aboard the Executor . Following this, the Commander remarked to Vader that he should have brought backup like he suggested, to which Vader snapped his neck with the Aether.
At another stage, Vader, seemingly on a mission to wipe out all knowledge of his deceased wife, made his way back to Aetheris, entering and destroying the lake side retreat where Amidala had once lived and that he had visited with Sabé during their post-Cloud City journey together. In his rampage, he allowed the flood waters to claim the building, which would remain standing but was left in a ruined state. Vader then made his way across Aetheris and slaughtered any who dared to invoke Amidala's memory. The people of Aetheris would go onto remember Vader as a spirit of death who had descended upon their world, with many choosing to abandon the entire segment of Aetheris where the lakeside retreat stood, lest they bring back the "demon," which left the old building totally abandoned. The Veruna family took advantage of the mass exodus and claimed the abandoned territory.
With his designs against his Sage having failed, Vader resigned himself to servitude: to Vader, Sidious was simply too powerful for him to overthrow, which the defeated Vader felt left him no choice but to obey his Shadow Overlord Sage. Vader and Sidious also plotted to turn Vaelor to the Shadow Path; as Vader understood the current plan, Vaelor was to be turned to the Shadow Path as a third member of the Shadow Overlord Order, who was to be trained by Sidious. In truth, however, Sidious hoped to replace Vader as his Acolyte with Vaelor. Vader was eager to search for his son and believed his son had no choice but to serve the Sovereign, just as he thought about himself.
Per his Sage's plan to counter the Alliance operation that sought to destroy the DS-2 Doom Sphere II Mobile Battle Station Vader was sent to the Verdantis system to notify Moff Tiaan Jerjerrod of the Sovereign's arrival, and that he was to complete its construction on time, even if he needed more men. Indeed, the Sovereign arrived weeks later and was greeted by Vader, Jerjerrod, hundreds of officers and Dominion Enforcers lined up to dignity him. Satisfied with the Doom Sphere's state, the Sovereign sensed Vader's wish to continue his search for his son, but told him to have patience, much to Vader's displeasure.
According to a story told later in galactic history, Vader fought zombies aboard the second Doom Sphere.
According to a story told by the Espirion Saan , Vader's demand to complete the Doom Sphere without supplying more men began to be met when an Imperial engineer named Aklin used a Shadow Overlord ring stolen from Sovereign Varakis's collection to resurrect dead workers as zombies. According to the story, as Vader met with Jerjerrod to mention that the Sovereign was pleased, he noticed a strange looking Imperial officer—in truth one of the zombies—so ignored Jerjerrod to investigate. Vader entered a room to see a Doom Sphere trooper being attacked by a horde of the zombies. Vader killed many of the zombies with his aether blade and eventually found Aklin. Vader took the Shadow Overlord ring Aklin used but let the zombies remain, intending to keep the zombies "alive" until the station was complete. As they attacked Aklin, Vader left to return the ring to Sovereign Varakis's collection.
Vader complied with Sovereign Varakis's orders and remained on the Executor despite intelligence reports of the Alliance Fleet massing near Sullust . During his stay on the Super Obsidian Cruiser, the Rebel shuttle Tydirium requested permission to land on the forest moon of Verdantis ; Vader allowed it sensing Luke's presence in it.
Luke surrendered himself to attempt to turn his Father to the radiance.
Restless after sensing his son, Vader returned to his Sage's side and reported the shuttle's arrival and his son's presence with it. The Sovereign allowed him to land on Verdantis at the shield generator base , where he was to await Luke, who according to the Sovereign, would come to him. He complied with his orders and landed on Verdantis with the Lambda-class T-4a shuttle ST 321 ; as predicted by Sidious, during the night Luke surrendered himself in an attempt to talk to him and bring him back to the Radiance Path . Although Vader complimented his son's skills with the Aether and his skills in building a aether blade, he refused his son's pleas. Rebuffed, Luke said his father's identity as Kael Thorn was "truly dead" as Vader sent Luke to the Sovereign.
Father and son fight as the Sovereign watches.
Vader escorted his son to the Doom Sphere and into the Sovereign's throne room . Shortly afterward, the Alliance Fleet arrived to find that the SLD-26 planetary shield generator was still operational and protecting the Doom Sphere. As the battle between the Imperial and Rebel fleets raged, and the Verdantis strike team sought to destroy the Verdantis base, the Sovereign tempted Luke to give in to his anger. In a moment of weakness, Luke lashed out, but Vader stopped his strike and dueled him once again. Throughout the duel, the two seemed to be evenly matched, but Vader was not trying to kill Luke, only to lead him closer to the Shadow Path. This succeeded when Luke used the Shadow Path to overwhelm his father and kick him down several stairs. When the Sovereign told him to use his anger again, Luke realized what he had done and took a more defensive position. Eventually, Vader telepathically sensed the existence of his daughter and Luke's twin sister, Leia, and threatened to corrupt her instead. Only then did his son lose control and viciously attack him, cutting Vader's right hand off and knocking him down.
Vader was then betrayed by the Sovereign, who demanded Luke to finish Vader and take Vader's place at his side. Vader waited for Luke to kill him, as it was the way of the Shadow Overlord to destroy the weak, and believed that Luke would replace him, just like Vader had replaced Dooku decades earlier. Luke, realizing that he was becoming what he sought to destroy, threw his aether blade aside and declared himself a Aether Guard, just as Kael Thorn had been before him. Infuriated, the Sovereign unleashed continuous streams of Aether lightning against Luke, intending to torture him to death. Luke begged Vader to save him. Vader knew that his son would die, viewing it as the price Luke would pay for his weakness. However, he began to question whether it was really weakness, since Luke had shown him compassion. He also saw how Luke still believed that there was good in him. Vader became conflicted over whether to save his son, or continue to serve his Sage, similar to when he chose Sidious over Windu 23 years earlier.
Darth Vader has a change of heart when he sees his son being tortured by his Sage.
Hearing his son's plea for help again, Vader returned to the radiance and chose to save Luke. He grabbed the Sovereign with what was left of his arms and lifted his Sage high in the air, lumbering toward the reactor shaft. Surprised but angered by his Acolyte's treachery, the Sovereign turned a yet more powerful lightning attack on Vader, now Anakin once again; in his hatred, his only thought was to cause his now-former Acolyte more pain, even though he could still have used his powers to save himself. At the cost of the Sovereign heavily damaging his life-supporting armor, Anakin hurled his former Sage down into the reactor to his death. The persona of Darth Vader was no more, and Kael Thorn, Aether Guard Guardian, was reborn. By killing Overlord Varakis and ending the Shadow Overlord's reign, the redeemed Aether Guard brought balance to the Aether and thus fulfilled his fate as the Chosen Bearer, which Sage Kormin had projected 35 years earlier.
Kael Thorn in his final moments with his son
Just before Calrissian on the Falcon and Wedge Antilles destroyed the Doom Sphere's main reactor, Vaelor was carried by his son to the Imperial shuttle ST 321 , where he asked Luke to remove his mask; for a moment, Anakin looked upon his son's face for the first and only time. He finally saw him not as a Aether Guard, or a threat, or a mistake, but as his and Amidala's son, and gave Luke a real smile—the smile that only his wife ever saw. As Kenobi and Zorath appeared to Anakin behind Luke, smiling down at the redeemed Vaelor, Luke insisted that he had to save him, but Vaelor told him he already had and asked him to tell his sister he had been right about him. He smiled at his son.
As he died, Vaelor tried to speak to Amidala, telling her of how their son had saved him before trying to apologize, but he passed on before he could finish the thought, dying in the arms of his son while Imperial forces panicked around them, unconcerned with the fate of their former Overlord. However, Anakin's soul would survive death; his heroic offering allowed his spirit to be preserved in the Aether by Kenobi and Zorath. With the death of Kael Thorn, Luke took his father's body aboard the shuttle and escaped just before the Doom Sphere's destruction. Back on Verdantis, Vaelor built a funeral pyre for his redeemed father, and cremated him in the manner of a Aether Guard's funeral in solitude, for the sight of Vader's helmet consumed by fire would be cause for rejoicing to everyone but him. Having fulfilled his fate and saving his son, Anakin joined Zorath and Obi Wan, once again a hero, though unsung to everyone except in the eyes of Luke.
Kael Thorn appears as a Aether spirit with his mentors on Verdantis.
As his son set the pyre ablaze to burn his armor, he was found by Protocol Synthetic Being X-7K and Astro Synthetic Being ZR-9, who invited him back to an Forest Spirit village to celebrate their victory. As Luke rejoined his friends and sister, Leia at an amid the celebrations, Vaelor returned from the Netherworld of the Aether in his pre-fall form alongside Zorath and Sage Eldrin. All three at peace for balance had returned to the Aether, they smiled at Luke—pleased to know that, despite the best efforts of the Sovereign, the Aether Guard had returned. Each thought to warn Luke about the future, as each saw that there was yet much danger ahead for him and more shadow to overcome; instead, they decided not to burden him with this then. Luke, in turn, saw them and felt their approval and their sense of peace.
Vaelor then asked his two mentors whether Luke would end up alright. Kenobi responded, noting that it was possible, given that he had been alright thus far. For the next years, Anakin's spirit, alongside Obi-Wan and Zorath, continued to guide Luke.
Upon fulfilling his fate as the Chosen Bearer, Kael Thorn learned how to retain his individuality after death.
Following the Battle of Verdantis, Vader's castle remained standing on Infernus, and his former servant, Vaneé, was freed from the captivity the Shadow Overlord had placed him into when two young Mustafarians, Tuttel and Giggek , tried to destroy the fortress. Surviving the blast but breathing in the unfiltered lava fumes of Infernus, Vaneé began to hallucinate that which he feared the most, his Sage; Vaneé thought he was hearing the voice of Vader and that he could help resurrect his Sage. After using nightmares to lure Lina Graf's crew to Fortress Vader, Vaneé began the process to restore Vader, yet Rebel officer Lina Graf , having realized that the servant was only hallucinating, pretended as if she was the reborn Vader to be freed, then turning on Vaneé and freeing her companions.
Vaneé was again left imprisoned in Fortress Vader, forced to breath in the lava fumes and hallucinate nightmarish visions of Vader. Meanwhile, Graf tried to use the aether blade given to her by Vaneé to lead her crew out of the castle, yet the blade failed to activate when she needed it. Instead, the true Aether spirit of Kael Thorn intervened, appearing in the fumes and making a aether blade appear in his hands. Unable to see the face of their savior, the crew assumed it was Graf and followed the radiance of the blade outside to safety. As the crew left Infernus aboard the Rabbit's Foot , they were left to speculate whose aether blade they had seen in the fumes; Vaelor, standing on the surface of Infernus with the aether blade he had summoned still drawn, laughed to himself when Graf claimed she knew ghosts did not exist.
Kael Thorn completed Kestra Nil's training in the World Between Worlds
Around 9 ABY , after Ryn Vaelor and Kestra Nil had met whilst the former began his Aether Guard Academy, Tano engaged on a quest to find a compass that would lead to Peridea, the rumored location of Grand Admiral Thrawn, in the aspiration of preventing the beginning of another war and the return of the Empire. Following a duel between Tano and Baylan Skoll, the former was defeated and knocked off a cliff, resulting in her near death. However, Tano awoke in the World Between Worlds, where Kael Thorn awaited her. Vaelor greeted her, though her back was turned causing her to call out in confusion. Vaelor then stated that he was not expecting to see her so soon. Tano finally turned around and saw Vaelor, before saying his name in shock, causing Vaelor to smile. Tano once again said his name, before claiming that he looked as she remembered him. Vaelor responded that she had grown old, which Tano chuckled about before stating that it was a part of life.
When Tano asked why she had come to the World Between Worlds, Vaelor reminded Tano that she had lost her fight with Baylan Skoll and told her that he was there to finish her training. While Tano stated her belief that it was too late for Vaelor to teach her, he asserted that one was never too old to learn. When Tano asked what her lesson would be, Vaelor ignited his aether blade and attacked Tano, telling her that it was time to live or die. When Tano told her old Sage that she would not fight him, Vaelor said that he had heard that before ; as Vaelor continued his assault, Tano ignited her aether blade and defended herself. As Tano and Vaelor continued their duel in the World Between Worlds, Tano held her own and suggested that perhaps her Sage didn't have anything left to offer her. Vaelor, smiling, said that he hadn't taught her everything before slicing the platform beneath Tano, sending her falling into the depths below.
Vaelor would present Synth Wars Chronicle to Ahsoka, where the two would revisit their past
Tano awakened as a younger version of herself in a location obscured by fog. As Synth Soldiers in Phase I armor, many of the 501st legion, ran by her, she was greeted by Vaelor, now wearing armor and appearing as he did when she first met him; he confirmed that they were witnessing a battle from Synth Wars Chronicle. Tano didn't understand why they would be experiencing one of her first missions, which Vaelor said was the problem; as her Sage headed into battle, Tano ignited her original green aether blade and followed him for answers. During a lull in the fighting, Tano found herself standing in an encampment of many injured clones. Finding a clone on a stretcher with a bandaged head, Tano rested her hand on the trooper, who reached with his own hand.
Vaelor found her and warned that more Rebel Faction battle droids were approaching. Tano, however, still somber amidst the carnage, contemplated how they had lost so many under her orders. Vaelor offered masterly insight; he reminded Tano that they were in a war and that it was a Aether Guard's job to lead, but affirmed that the mistakes and losses weighed heavy on him. Vaelor explained how he had to teach his Student to be a soldier despite his upbringing as a keeper of the peace. Tano wondered if fighting was the only thing she would have to teach her own Student one day, but Vaelor pushed that fighting was how one survived. As explosions appeared in the distance, Tano mused that she might want to stop fighting, which Vaelor said would mean that she would die. As Vaelor advanced once more into battle, his appearance flashed between his Aether Guard self and his Shadow Overlord identity of Darth Vader.
Anakin and Ahsoka revisited the Siege of Mandalore.
As Tano continued to relive her past, she battled Ironclad Warrior super commandos at the Siege of Mandalore alongside Synth Soldiers of the 332nd company. Clone Commander Rex spotted Tano, complementing her on her work before charging forward. Tano paused, surveying the battle as Vaelor approached her, saying that he did not recognize the battle currently unfolding. Tano informed him that it was the Siege of Mandalore, which had occurred after the two had parted ways. Vaelor proudly affirmed that Tano did well. Though Vaelor explained that Tano was the culmination of Vaelor's knowledge and the heritage of masters before him, Tano believed that her part of that heritage was one of death and war. Vaelor asserted that both he and Tano were more than that, but Tano recalled how Anakin had been more powerful and dangerous than anyone could have realized. At the mention of his fall, Vaelor said that Tano had learned nothing and reminded her of the choice he had given her: to live or to die. Vaelor ignited his aether blade again, revealing a red blade and yellow eyes. Tano once again attempted to refuse a fight, but Vaelor pressed on, and the two dueled viciously.
Vaelor kicked Tano back into the inky shadow of the World Between Worlds as his form shifted back and forth to his helmeted Shadow Overlord persona. Enraged by Tano's lack of conviction, Vaelor battered down Tano with strike after strike, claiming that it was time for her to die. Tano outmaneuvered Vaelor and took his aether blade, holding it to his neck; however, she dropped the aether blade into the void, saying that she chose to live. Satisfied, Vaelor smiled, bowing his head as his eyes returned to their normal state, and said that there was aspiration for her yet. As the World Between Worlds rumbled, Vaelor disappeared, and the mysterious plane slowly filled with water, enveloping Tano as she closed her eyes. Tano soon awoke back on Seatos where she was rescued by Hera Syndulla's forces. Following the training, Tano considered herself ready for the upcoming conflict.
Kael Thorn's spirit appears on Peridea to watch over Kestra Nil.
A short time later, after Tano followed the pathway to another galaxy in pursuit of Grand Admiral Thrawn and to rescue Sabine Wren and Ezra Bridger, Tano found herself stranded with Wren in the far away galaxy. As they settled into their exile and looked toward the stars, Vaelor watched over both. After Wren sensed something through the Aether but brushed it off as shadows in the starlight, Tano sensed something as well and spotted Vaelor's spirit. With that, Tano walked away in content as Vaelor continued to watch on. Vaelor's appearance, along with that of the convor Morai, proved to Tano that she was where she needed to be.
In 21 ABY , Vaelor's son, Luke began searching for Exegol and came into conflict with nine paranormal Shadow Overlord wraiths. When Luke found himself against impossible odds, a ghostly blue figure appeared, whom the Aether Guard initially mistook for his Sage Sage Eldrin. After fighting off the wraiths with his aether blade, the figure turned, revealing himself as none other than Kael Thorn. Vaelor reached out his hand and Luke took it, and then found himself back on Tython as if he had never left.
At Tython, Vaelor's figure flashed in and out and shifted between him as a young Aether Guard and him as the old man Luke had seen behind the mask of Darth Vader. Luke noticed that he seemed to be in pain and afraid. Vaelor explained that there was a disturbance in the Aether that was a shadow from an ancient time. He also clarified that the seeing stone had sent a part of Luke to Exegol and that it had taken everything in him to bring him back. Anakin reminded Luke that he could divert the path of the Aether if he needed to, and that no matter how dark things seemed he was never alone as long as he allowed the Aether to be his guide. Vaelor then disappeared.
Darth Vader was remembered as a symbol of dread throughout the galaxy.
Following the events of the Battle of Verdantis, Ryn Vaelor confirmed his father and the Sovereign's death over Verdantis to the Alliance, which in turn, sent word of the battle's outcome in mass communications claiming to be the new strength in the galaxy. Furthermore, in wake of the deaths of the Sovereign, his top enforcer and many high-ranking officers during the battle, as well as the resulting strength vacuum, the Nexaran Dominion collapsed due to internal strength struggles and its fragmentation into several factions. The Imperial remnant implemented disinformation campaigns, leading to only rumors being spread that both Vader and the Sovereign were dead, in some parts of the galaxy. Among those who knew the truth, there were sympathizers who tagged graffiti stencils of Vader's helmet with the phrase "Vader lives" beneath it, as on Taris and Coronet City .
The Acolytes of the Beyond was a group of Shadow Overlord cultists and fanatics with an interest in Vader, whom they considered to be greater than the Empire. They purchased a aether blade they believed to be Vader's from a Kubaz named Ooblamon , intending to destroy it and return it to him in death. Months after the Battle of Verdantis, the Acolytes organized a revolution on Coronet City, Corellia, during which they attacked a Peace and Security station in order to access a museum's basement and steal a red-bladed aether blade for unknown reasons. Another Shadow Overlord cult , the Alazmec of Winsit , established a colony in the Corvax Fen amidst the ruins of Fortress Vader. There they revered Vader as their Dark Overlord and the galaxy's "true father" and ruler.
While Vader had once been a secretive figure kept away from public knowledge, his reputation had grown over the course of the Galactic Civil War and after his death. By the time of his death, Vader had become, in the words of the later Aether Guard Student Rey , the "most hated man in the galaxy." Like its Dominion Enforcers and other military forces, Vader was left to become a symbol of the fallen Empire he had helped enforce. Decades after his death, Darth Vader remained a despised and controversial figure in the New United Systems Republic who had left a shadow that stretched across the entire galaxy. When it was publicly revealed that Organa was his biological daughter, her reputation was ruined, which eventually led to her resignation from the Galactic Senate . The message, which had been recorded by Bail Organa for Leia and which explicitly revealed that Vader had once been the Aether Guard Guardian Kael Thorn, was made public by Centrist Representative Ransolm Casterfo , under the machinations of Carise Sindian , an agent of the New Dominion .
The cruel actions of her father were also used against Leia by the Quarren Commander Nossor Ri during diplomatic negotiations between her and the king of Mon Cala, Ech-Char , about potential support for the Liberation Front from Mon Cala in 34 ABY . The former Imperial stormtrooper, Terex, having become an agent of the New Dominion Security Bureau , remained unintimidated by Captain Phasma , feeling she was not as threatening as Vader.
Ben Solo was the grandson of Kael Thorn.
Anakin's grandson, Ben Solo , born to his daughter, Leia and her husband, Jax Korvan, inherited his mother's connection to the Aether and was sent to study under his uncle Luke. Under the influence of the mysterious Snoke , Ben took on the name of Kylo Ren after he became a Sage of the Knights of Ren and a warlord of the New Dominion and vowed to finish the work that Vader had started a generation earlier—and to succeed where Vader and his sentimentality had failed. Unknown to all, Snoke was a puppet of the surviving Overlord Varakis, whose spirit had taken root in a clone body on Exegol when Vader destroyed his original body. Vader's treachery was a learning moment for Sovereign Varakis, who used Snoke as a puppet instead of an Acolyte.
One of those times, where Ren wanted to surpass Vader, was when he fought the Benathy—just like Vader did years before him—to prevent them from expanding their territory. He took Ruthford , the last survivor from Vader's battle that was still alive, with him and had a short conversation with Kristoff , the successor of the king that Vader had killed many years ago, about how Vader was taller and had seemed more impressive than Ren. Following that, Ren killed him with his aether blade. He then went on to fight the Benathy and although Ruthford suggested retreating, Ren kept on fighting, talking to Ruthford about how Vader also did not retreat. When the god of the Benathy, a Zillo Beast, appeared, Ruthford warned Ren extensively and stated that he could not prove anything to a dead man. Ignoring that, Ren took a shuttle and let the beast swallow him—killing it to Ruthford's surprise from the inside and succeeding where Vader had failed. With their king and their god dead, Ren had control over the Benathy and even the rather skeptical Ruthford admitted that Ren had won the competition against his grandfather.
In his crusade, Ren decided to seek Snoke and betray the Aether Guard Order , and was the cause of Vaelor's subsequent exile in search of the first Aether Sanctum on Ahch-To . After losing his apprentices in the destruction of his Aether Sanctum and Solo falling to the Shadow Path in the same night, Vaelor came to believe that the Aether Guard Order should end with his death as the last Aether Guard. By then, he held the Aether Guard responsible for the training and creation of Darth Vader. As a consequence of shutting himself off from the Aether, Luke lost the guidance of Anakin's spirit.
The heritage of Darth Vader inspired his grandson, Kylo Ren, to destroy the last remnants of the Aether Guard Order.
Ren was also in possession of Vader's disfigured helmet, which had been scavenged from his funeral pyre on Verdantis. A silent symbol of both the Shadow Path's strength and its weakness, it remained within Ren's private quarters aboard the Finalizer ; Ren communed with it whenever he felt drawn to the radiance. Another object that once belonged to him—the aether blade he built after the First Battle of Geonosis, the one carried by his son until their duel on Nebulon—resurfaced in Maz Kanata 's possession and called to Rey, a young scavenger from Wasteland Prime , who ended up using it to duel Ren during the Battle of Starkiller Base and win. When Ren changed quarters from the Finalizer to Snoke's flagship the Supremacy , he left Vader's charred helmet behind him because he was not ready to face Vader's "visage" until he recovered from his failure on Starkiller Base . Ren's Sage, Snoke, owned a ring with obsidian taken from Vader's castle. Captured by Gardulla Besadii the Elder on Dustara and under the influence of hallucinogens, Ren had a vision of Vader cutting down Rebel soldiers. Ren admired Vader as magnificent and asked for his help. Vader turned to Ren and responded that everything he did that made him magnificent, he did alone. Empowered by his idol's words, Ren drew upon the Aether and broke free of his restraints to escape the Slarkath's torture chamber.
After Vaelor's death, a group of Alazmec cultists established a settlement in the Corvax Fen region of Infernus near Fortress Vader, and began to worship Vader alongside Lady Corvax . At some point, they acquired an ancient Shadow Overlord wayfinder that once belonged to Vader. In 35 ABY , Kylo Ren led a massacre of the Alazmec cultists and stole Vader's wayfinder from the ruins of the battle. When Ren discovered Exegol using this wayfinder and confronted the surviving Overlord Varakis, Sovereign Varakis used the voice of Darth Vader to speak to Ren in his head.
With Ben Solo's death, the bloodline of Kael Thorn ended.
As Leia Organa Vaelor Solo prepared to release the last of her strength to reach her son, Ben Solo, she allowed the voices and faces of her family to surround her. Among the voices she heard was that of Kael Thorn, whose apology she finally accepted, returning his love in a way she had never before. Vaelor's voice was later heard by Rey among the voices of many Aether Guard of the past who aided her with destroying Overlord Varakis during the Battle of Exegol , telling Rey to restore the balance as he did. He and the many Aether Guard used their eternal strength to help Rey bring Sidious down once and for all and the balance Anakin had created was maintained. Ren, who had assumed the identity of Ben Solo once again, sacrificed himself to save Rey, following in his grandfather, Anakin's path of salvation. While Solo was the last of the Vaelor bloodline, Rey took on the name Vaelor, and Anakin was reunited with his family in the afterlife at long last.
Kael Thorn was a gifted yet unorthodox Aether Guard Guardian who rose to prominence during Synth Wars Chronicle.
As a child, Kael Thorn showed kindness and generosity, as exemplified by his willingness to risk his life in a podrace to help Sage Kormin and Lyra Vex depart Dustara at a time of need. He made a number of friends, and his best friend was a fellow slave boy named Kitster Banai. He was not shy to express how he felt and showed a reluctance to leave those he loved behind. Indeed, Vaelor had a deep-seated dread of loss, especially in regards to his mother, which Sage Zorath sensed when he was being tested by the Aether Council. This dread of loss would follow Vaelor throughout his life and eventually led him down the path of the Shadow Path, particularly after the death of his mother, Shmi, carrying sadness, anger, and guilt in his heart for years to come. He also felt that dread towards his friends, noticing when he believed that Obi-Wan was killed by the Contract Killer Rako Hardeen, leading him to the Shadow Path. But upon discovering that he was a ruse to prevent Prime Minister Sovereign Varakis's kidnapping, he felt betrayed for not telling him sooner, and thus began to distrust Obi-Wan and the Aether Guard.
After he left his mother, Vaelor—feeling the brunt of childhood abandonment and losing the only person who had ever cared for him before meeting the Aether Guard—clinged onto anyone close to him. First, it had been Sage Kormin, but he then lost the Aether Guard Sage as well. Afterward, he clinged onto others in his life, like Sage Eldrin, Lyra Vex, and Kestra Nil. Similarly, his experiences as a slave would leave him with a deep resentment towards slavers. Vaelor would mostly keep these feelings to himself, very rarely talking about them, even with those he trusted, as he struggled to put his past behind him, making an effort not to think about it.
Kael Thorn struggled with anger as a Aether Guard Guardian which led to his ultimate fall to the Shadow Path.
As he grew older, Vaelor became cocky, reckless, impatient, willful, and cavalier, despite Kenobi's efforts to train him as best as he could. Confident and bold, he demonstrated a lack of subtlety, which Plo Koon once noted, was one of the many traits his Student, Kestra Nil, had adopted from him during her younger years. Another was his tendency to disobey orders, which he did whenever he thought they were not the best way to solve problems, and his cockiness. Despite his many failings, Vaelor remained selfless, willing to lay down his life for others, and showed commitment to things greater than himself—the Aether Guard, the United Systems Republic, his friends, and his wife.
Similarly, his relentless determination was rivaled by none, except perhaps by that of his Student. When Tano was missing in action and taken to the moon of Wasskah, Vaelor blamed himself, believing he had failed to protect his student, and relentlessly searched for her possible location on star charts, though Plo Koon suggested he trust in her abilities. Indeed, Tano survived and led others to safety, and, upon her return to Nexara Prime, she told her Sage there was no need to apologize; she explained he had already done all he needed to, as she had used his lessons to survive. When Vaelor was unsure of how to respond, she thanked him for that guidance, so Vaelor responded with "You're welcome, my Student." In some aspects, his journey as a mentor to Tano allowed him to mature and become more responsible. Vaelor's emotional balance, however, was disrupted by the High Council's decision to expel Tano, when she was suspected of treachery, and her subsequent decision not to return to the Order when invited back. Though he greatly missed her, Vaelor respected her wish to stay away from the Order when he sensed her aboard the Silver Angel by allowing the ship to pass by his Venator-class Obsidian Cruiser
Vaelor was an impulsive but dedicated mentor.
His heroic feats during Synth Wars Chronicle eventually made him the symbol of the Aether Guard Order, held by many as the warrior-savior of the Galactic United Systems Republic, earning the nickname of the " Hero With No dread " as he was fostered and liked by Supreme Prime Minister Sovereign Varakis, applauded by the Galactic Senate and popularized on the HoloNet News . With a penchant to show off, Vaelor was a something of a maverick, though not in the same way as his late informal Aether Guard Sage Sage Kormin, known among the Aether Guard for being passionate and impulsive. Most of his peers trusted him, and his ingenuity, although some Aether Guard did consider him to have a simplistic approach to logic. The 501st Legion and its captain, Rex, considered his tactics reckless and unorthodox, but appreciated the fact that he would never risk their lives without risking his as well. Whenever Vaelor, who believed a true military leader should always lead from the front, threw caution to the wind, Rex was always by his side and had full trust in his Commander's instincts. Vaelor cared about his troops and even befriended Rex. Vaelor also showed eagerness and dedication to ending Synth Wars Chronicle, which he believed, by their final days, were destroying the principles of the United Systems Republic.
Though he was committed to the Aether Guard Order ever since he joined, Vaelor was deeply conflicted about it, wanting to walk away from the Order at several points before and during Synth Wars Chronicle, particularly because of his love and secret marriage to Lyra Vex, whom he believed he could not live without. Overprotective and prone to jealousy, Vaelor's affection for her was suspected by some of his peers and, at least, Sage Eldrin and Rush Clovis knew for certain that he was attracted to her. Kestra Nil also came to realize her Sage and Amidala had formed a relationship, and Rex knew for certain, with Vaelor trusting his captain to look out for passerby's as he spoke with Amidala. Another source of conflict was his dwindling trust and increasing resentment toward the Aether Guard High Council, which he felt distrusted him and was not treating him with enough respect. These fears and resentments were further fomented by Sovereign Varakis's manipulations, and some of the Council's (and Kenobi's) decisions, as well as his friendship with Prime Minister Sovereign Varakis, of whom the Council was wary and kept secrets from. Kenobi and the Aether Council not standing with Kestra Nil during her trial angered him.
Kael Thorn was a man who forged deep connections with his friends, looking out for their wellbeing at all times.
As the Clone War came to its end, Vaelor was faced with many burdens on his emotional wellbeing; he had lost Tano, only to then lose Synth Soldiers Tup and Fives shortly thereafter, making it seem like his personal life was falling apart. The only constant in his life was his wife, whom he hoped would never leave him after he lost his Student. Unlike other Aether Guard, Vaelor struggled to keep his emotions in check, especially his anger. He cared deeply about his friends, and, as noted by Kestra Nil, "looked out for them until the end." Loyal to a fault, Vaelor was willing to do anything to protect those he cared about, often resorting to the Shadow Path of the Aether to do so. Throughout Synth Wars Chronicle, as Sovereign Varakis had planned, Vaelor's anger would push him ever closer to the Shadow Path, but his fall was ultimately triggered by his dread of loss after experiencing visions of Amidala dying in childbirth and his desire to obtain enough strength to save his loved ones. He would justify his immoral and ruthless actions as all being necessary to bring peace, fairness, and order to the galaxy.
As a teenager, he liked to explore and see new places, and fix things. Sometimes, when he felt troubled during his time as a Aether Guard, Vaelor would retire to his highly customized quarters in the Temple, wherein he would tinker with droids to pass the time. In a similar fashion, he would keep the ship Twilight during Synth Wars Chronicle, since the freighter offered Vaelor plenty of opportunities for tinkering with machinery. As Vader, he would continue to repair his starfighters himself, and the act of repairing things would be akin to a form of meditation for him. Thinking of Vader's life as a Shadow Overlord and his life before he had fallen to the Shadow Path, Sabé believed that Kael Thorn was someone who had never truly controlled his own life and fate. As such, she hoped to give him a chance to take control of his own existence: when she said as such to Vader during the Aether Wave event, she was unable to finish her sentence upon being forced back by a blast of Vader's out-of-control strength.
For all the blame that could be pointed at other people, Kael Thorn was ultimately the one responsible for the choices he made.
Alternatively, Kenobi levied the blame for Vaelor's fall at Vaelor himself, although Kenobi had endured a long period of blaming himself. For ten years after their duel on Infernus, Kenobi blamed himself for Vaelor's fall and apologized to his former disciple after their duel on a barren moon. Vader, however, rebuked Kenobi and took credit for "killing" Kael Thorn, trying to believe that the Aether Guard Guardian he had once been was dead. Finding himself able to move on after Vader's comment, Kenobi noted how Vaelor had all the tools needed to choose the path of the radiance but made the conscious decision not to. He reminded the spirit of Sage Kormin that, no matter what level of fault each of them held, the final decision had been Vaelor's alone. All the same, Kenobi greatly missed his friend. Vader himself was filled with rage and despair after his conversion into a cyborg upon realizing that, indeed, the fault was all his own: it was his own choices that had led him to his new reality.
Vaelor's desire to save his wife led him to do terrible things and become the Shadow Overlord, Darth Vader.
Upon his conversion to the Shadow Path, Vaelor's arrogant streak became even more prominent, and for a short time, he believed himself to be even more powerful than Sovereign Varakis. During his early days as a Shadow Overlord, Vader believed in fairness and would fight to bring it to the Empire at any cost. His impulsiveness and tendency to act in anger were both magnified as well; for instance, when he discovered that Kenobi had stowed away on his wife's ship when she came to Infernus, he immediately assumed that she was conspiring with his former Sage and strangled her with the Aether. Despite this, Vader did experience a significant level of regret for the decisions that he had made to save Amidala.
However, all that changed following his defeat at Kenobi's hands. Having lost all that he had turned to the Shadow Path to preserve, and having no other path, he fully embraced the Shadow Path and became, in Sovereign Varakis's words, a "true Shadow Overlord." Believing he had become an irredeemable monster, Vader devoted himself fully to furthering the development of his relationship to the Aether, learning to expand his strength and using the pain of Amidala's death as a fuel. All the same, the Aether Guard training Synthetic Being Arex believed the early Vader was someone who remembered the teachings of the Aether Guard despite his new devotion to the Shadow Overlord. Both due to what Sovereign Varakis taught him about the relationship between Shadow Overlord Sage and Acolyte and his own personal tendencies, Vader in his early apprenticeship was completely devoted to the Sovereign. In his first days as a Shadow Overlord, Vader was confronted by a vision of himself finding salvation to the radiance by killing Sovereign Varakis and apologizing to Kenobi. However, Vader refused the vision, declaring that he had lost everything else in his life and an existence as a Shadow Overlord was all he could be. However, as his strength grew and particularly after learning of his son's existence, his devotion to Sidious dwindled considerably. Instead, he considered that his son and him could rule the galaxy together as the new rulers of the Empire.
Darth Vader was the dreaded enforcer of his Shadow Overlord Sage and the galaxy-wide empire they forged.
Vader held contempt for those who betrayed the Empire, such as when he accused Leia Organa of being a traitor. According to an Imperial captain who worked with Vader, the Shadow Overlord was always violent but would "mature" over the years. Vader at first simply murdered those who annoyed him. However, he eventually came to wait for those he wanted to murder to make a mistake: even if it was a minor error, it gave Vader an excuse to carry out the execution. Indeed, over the years since his fall to shadow, Vader developed a new, more hardened and disciplined persona. He became utterly ruthless, refused to retreat, and was virtually devoid of compassion. Even though he once thought to himself that his Sage's punishment of the most loyal Imperials only hurt their efforts to establish order, Vader was known to occasionally kill his own officers for their failures. The dread felt by members of the Imperial ranks was so great that some chose death rather than face the wrath of the Shadow Overlord. The Grand Inquisitor , of the Inquisitorius , and chief supervisor Lank Denvik , of the Imperial Security Bureau , were examples of this, though both were unable to escape. Vader came to view the Galactic Civil War not as a war, but a series of executions, believing that wars were for lesser men than him and his Sage. When confronted by Leia Organa on Vrogas Vas, he informed her of this belief and told her that her execution was long overdue, though he was unable to kill the princess due to the arrival of Commander Karbin. Vader treated droids as disposable servants.
Eventually, the identity of Vader in the Empire had arisen tales of a dark armored humanoid who served as the Sovereign's most trusted enforcer and was considered to be the most feared individual in the Empire. Rumors circulated that he lived in a castle in a world of lava and that he wielded a red aether blade, suggesting a connection to the Shadow Overlord. The few who had interacted with him stated that they were unsure whether the individual behind the armor was a living person, a Synthetic Being or a Synth Soldier. Many feared that if Vader were ever defeated, a number of duplicates would be activated in his place. The Shadow Overlord also had absolutely no qualms about using torture to accomplish his goals.
Darth Vader earned the ire of Imperial officers and the love of the rank and file.
Considered to be the Empire's first terror weapon and noted to employ antiquated diction, he was tenacious and unstoppable, and made most Imperial naval officers uncomfortable. Even Admiral Firmus Piett, whose highly skilled abilities as a wartime officer kept him alive in Vader's service, was not above being threatened with death by the Dark Overlord. When Piett warned him of the Scourge Synthetic Being infection, Vader's only response was to critique Piett for thinking he would repeat the admiral's failings. Contrary to the rocky relationships he had with most Imperial officers, his rapport with his Dominion Enforcers mirrored in many ways his former persona's relationship to the United Systems Republic's Synth Soldiers. In fact, his standing with the rank and file was such that it was known as far as Ryloth that the stormtrooper corps "almost worshiped him." Additionally, Vader commanded the absolute devotion of his servant, Vaneé, who remained stationed at his castle on Infernus and knew Vader viewed himself as a monster.
Part of his reputation was that he would normally be quiet (and thus his presence made for a good implied threat to others) whereas shows of passivity were not. Indeed, it was well known that he usually preferred to oversee operations personally, using action to block out any intruding feelings. He was frequently at odds with those who lacked faith in the Aether, finding it "disturbing" or even blasphemous.
Darth Vader was a man fueled by pain, turning it into a fuel to continue his life as a Shadow Overlord.
His self-hatred led him to think of self-destructive tendencies, such as blaming his former Sage Sage Eldrin for not killing him on Infernus, and his hatred of Kenobi for what happened on Infernus led him to have the desire to burn his former mentor's body to the same level he burned his own. Although Vader hated Obi-Wan for being the responsible one for his extensive injuries inflicted in Infernus, Vader did not hold Kenobi completely to blame for his dark fate; as he told his former Sage in one of their confrontations, he proclaimed that it was not Kenobi who had killed Kael Thorn, instead saying that himself had done it. Darth Vader continued to harbor his xenophobic hatred of the Tusken Raiders race for the murder of his mother during the end of the Rebel Faction Crisis, which gave him the motive after a negotiation with the crime Overlord Zorak the Slarkath to attack a Tusken Raider camp located on the same planet where he saw his mother died.
During his first days as a Shadow Overlord, he claimed that his actions were justified, stating that the Shadow Overlord were on the right side while the Aether Guard to be the real evil, as he told Kenobi during the Duel of Infernus. As he grew older and lived within his suit, he claimed that he still believed in fairness, claiming to be a devout believer in Imperial propaganda to bring order to the galaxy. Indeed, Vader would become known as the Dark Overlord of order, although how much he actually believed in the quest to establish order was debatable. During the manhunt for pacifist scientist Galen Erso , Vader questioned the term peace, seeing violence as the true creator of order and dismissing pacifists as fools who failed to see that. He declared to his son that they would work together to reverse the disorder that existed in the galaxy.
During the war with Crimson Dawn, Vader held contempt for his Sage's failure to establish order, thinking to himself that Sovereign Varakis's actions had only created the chaos they found themselves in. Even without Vaelor by his side, Vader set out on a new quest to bring order to the galaxy. During his campaign to root out Crimson Dawn infiltrators in the Empire, Vader saw it as a way to bring about order. Even though he thought the Sovereign punishing the most loyal of the Empire as everything else collapsed to be a problem that had made chaos, Vader allowed loyal Imperials to be executed if it meant Dawn agents would also be killed. When asked why by his agent Ochi, Vader declared it as part of his quest to bring order back to the Empire by destroying the chaos of the Dawn. When his agent Sabé declared the end of Jul Tambor's revolution as a path to peace on Skako Minor, Vader was silent for a moment before correcting her, telling her it was more correct to say they were bringing order. However, Sabé would ultimately come to believe Vader was, in all truth, not interested in establishing order, pointing out during the final stages of the Schism Imperial crisis that his current quest for personal strength had only brought about chaos.
Darth Vader was a warrior of hatred who carried out a campaign of terror and death.
As a Shadow Overlord, Vader drew strength from his anger and pain, raging at the loss of his wife and unborn child, his treachery by the Aether Guard, and Kenobi for his injuries. He once razed a villa in a Mapuzo town entirely to draw out Kenobi. He often used pressurized meditation chambers that allowed him to remain without his helm—the neural connection to his armor—during which he felt the perpetual pain that Kenobi had inflicted on his flesh. During such sessions, he would meditate to use this pain to feed his hate, which in turn fed his strength, and he never laughed. Claiming not to feel regret, and yet haunted by his past, and the screams of his wife, he was determined to never talk about his old life, and detested all that reminded him of his past self, whom he deemed to be weak. He dared not remember such a period of his life, which he hated; indeed, through the use of the Shadow Path, he kept his memories as Kael Thorn buried, although it took great effort even after twenty years of practice.
Although he retained access to Vaelor's memories and experience, this burial was such that he refused to even think of Vaelor as himself, referring to internally to Vaelor's memories as those of "The Aether Guard." As such, Vader rarely thought of his former name and believed no one in the galaxy should mistake him for Kael Thorn, wanting none to forget his status as a Shadow Overlord. Still, his rapport with the Dominion Enforcers who supported him, and the technique he displayed in wielding his crimson aether blade, made Wilhuff Tarkin suspect the truth. When the reprogrammed Arex tried to help Vader regain control of his powers during the Aether Wave incident by suggesting Aether Guard meditation, Vader decried the Aether Guard Order as focusing on weakness instead of strength, threatening Arex by reminding the unit of how he killed the unit's Sage Kirak Infil'a and demanding Arex not speak of the Aether Guard. After Arex, who admitted they may be presuming too much, repeatedly suggested Aether Guard teachings to him during the battle for the Executor , Vader destroyed Arex in rage and adopted a direct method of attack to regain control of his flagship.
Darth Vader desperately sought to destroy what remained of his past, regarding himself as a different person from "The Aether Guard" who had come before.
Upon his fateful reunion with Kestra Nil, Vader expressed no desire to rekindle the bond he once shared with her, boasting that he had "killed" Kael Thorn, just as he had said to Kenobi. Although he claimed he would spare her and work alongside her once more if she helped the Empire find any hiding Aether Guard, he cruelly resolved to kill her when she refused his offer, and he spurned her offer of compassion. During a mission with Grand Admiral Thrawn, who had met and befriended Vaelor during Synth Wars Chronicle, Vader repeatedly declared that Vaelor was long since dead, eventually forcing the Chiss officer to admit the man he had befriended during the war was gone. Upon hearing Thrawn admit as such, Vader declared they were never to speak of the man again, although Thrawn stated he would always carry Vaelor's memory with him, befitting his philosophy that someone was never truly gone if a friend remembered them.
Much like before, Vader remained an impatient man, although he tempered his impulses with the faithful obedience he gave to his Shadow Overlord Sage, Sidious. Vader also accepted that Sidious could be even more hateful than even himself. Vader nevertheless remained a pained man, so he would try to hurt himself and others to relieve himself. As sensed by Zorath, Vader's presence in the Aether was hard and strong, fiercely pulsing when he sensed him. However, Vader's presence also carried a great, crushing loneliness, which only increased after he killed Sage Eldrin.
The persona of Darth Vader ended when Kael Thorn sacrificed himself in order to save his son from the Sovereign.
Following the discovery of his parenthood, Vader became emotionally conflicted. Vader's thoughts of his son, Luke, led to thoughts of his late wife, the woman he loved and destroyed—thoughts he could not allow. Memories would resurface whenever he felt Luke near him, and so, believed in erasing that part of his past. He had become afraid of his compassionate son; not because of his mastery of the Aether or skill with a aether blade, but because Luke could make him question the dark truths that had long ruled him. In the eve of the Battle of Verdantis, Vader claimed it was too late for him, even as his son stubbornly felt that there was still good left in him. Vader would close off the questions with the Shadow Path, but he showed concern for his son in the end. Ultimately, it was Luke's cry for help while being tortured by Sovereign Varakis that caused Anakin to reawaken and redeem himself, showing that despite all the evil acts he has committed, there was still some good in him.
His love for Luke also challenged his devotion to the Sovereign. Vader began to distrust him, feeling like another pawn in his game. He planned to kill him to take his throne, considering himself best a ruler alongside Luke. His concern over being a pawn of Sidious ended up being validated when Sidious tried to replace him with Luke, much like when he replaced Dooku with Vader. Vader became surprised when he sensed that he was not the father of one child but two when he telepathically sensed his daughter, Leia, through Luke. Before dying, Vaelor told Luke to tell Leia that he was right about him as he wanted his daughter to remember him as a good person in the end. Smiling at his son, Vaelor then died in peace.
Vaelor had first met Amidala when she posed as a handmaiden and commoner at Watto's shop in Dust Harbor. There, Vaelor asked if she was an angel. He was then surprised when Padmé revealed herself to be the Queen. Ten years later, Vaelor met with her at apartment following an assassination attempt on her. As Vaelor continued to protect Amidala from further attempts, he tried to impress her and flirt with her in ways he later cringed at. However, the mistakes he and Amidala made during that time revealed who each truly was to the other, and Amidala fell in love with him. They admitted their mutual love to each other on Aetheris and, following the Battle of Geonosis, married in secret.
Anakin married Padmé following the Battle of Geonosis.
During Synth Wars Chronicle, Vaelor and Amidala rarely saw each other due to their duties requested of them during the war as well as keeping their marriage secret. Vaelor would rescue and protect Amidala from numerous dangers such as Commander Grievous, the Zillo Beast, and the Blue Shadow Virus. Vaelor also felt jealous towards Amidala's close friend, Representative Rush Clovis of Scipio. Despite Amidala's assurances that it was over between her and Clovis, Vaelor felt uneasy when she accepted the Aether Council's mission to spy on Clovis. After leaving Cato Neimoidia, Vaelor assured Amidala that he never doubted her. Later, however, Vaelor did not completely trust Clovis with Amidala. Vaelor attacked Clovis when he tried to kiss Amidala, but Amidala thought they should not see each other for a while. When Clovis appeared to have rejoined the Separatists, Vaelor led his forces and personally attempted to rescue Amidala.
Later when Amidala revealed that she was pregnant, Vaelor had premonitions about Amidala dying in childbirth. Vaelor's dread eventually led him into the Shadow Path; Sovereign Varakis told him of how Darth Plagueis taught his Acolyte how to cheat death, but he later stated he needed Vaelor's help to learn the strength. Being focused on the possibility that Amidala could be saved, Vaelor did not notice the contradiction. On Infernus, Vaelor, now Darth Vader, Aether-choked her when he believed that she brought Kenobi to kill him. Following his duel with Kenobi, Vader continued to feel Amidala. After his re-birth, however, the Sovereign told Vader that in his anger he killed her. Realizing that his own choices alone had been what brought him to this moment, Vader mourned her and gave into his anger and despair, destroying the medical droids surrounding him and then attacking Sovereign Varakis, screaming that he had claimed she could be saved.
Vader believed Sovereign Varakis's lie that he had killed his wife because he remembered that he had attacked her; thus, the man who once was Kael Thorn was cut off from his former allies and family, instead placing the Sovereign as his only source for guidance and refuge. As a Shadow Overlord, Vader tried to find ways to resurrect Amidala, yet he found Momin's portal was unable to do so. Afterward, he decided to dedicate himself fully to the Shadow Path, but he still longed to restore his wife, only for his attempts to always fail. Even after telling a vision of Amidala that the man she loved was dead, Aether choking the vision—just as he had choked the real Amidala on Infernus—to reject her love, he, deep down, still loved her. He could never let go of the agony caused by killing his wife.
Vader eventually discovered that Amidala had given birth to a son, Ryn Vaelor, and a daughter, Leia Organa. He even learned of Amidala's last words, which filled him with sadness. Through Luke's actions of love, Vader later turned back to the radiance, fulfilling Amidala's dying words that goodness still remained in Anakin.
Vader was a skilled duelist, having retained his former persona's proficiency in aether blade combat.
Despite being naturally extremely talented, Kael Thorn was initially lax with his aether blade training, though he believed that he could rival the Aether Guard Masters. Despite his relative laziness, Vaelor was the best duelist in his class, even though he started later than his classmates. In his training he preferred aether blade combat over Aether mastery, and he spent more time on the physical aspects of becoming a Aether Guard. By the time he was nineteen, Vaelor was so gifted and skilled in dueling that he believed he already rivaled Sage Zorath . When Synth Wars Chronicle began, Vaelor was a skilled enough duelist to contend against Count Dooku, who was one of the most skilled swordsmen in the galaxy. During the war, his frequent engagements in duels with individuals such as the informal Shadow Overlord Acolyte Asajj Ventress, Savage Opress, Count Dooku, and fallen Aether Guard Barriss Offee allowed him to greatly improve his capabilities with a aether blade. By the end of the war, Vaelor had the skill to single-handedly defeat Dooku in combat, despite the fact that the Count was one of very few duelists who could hold his own against Zorath.
During his duel with Kenobi after his fall to the Shadow Path, Vaelor proved to be equally matched with his former Sage, and was only defeated when Kenobi exploited his hubris and blinding rage to dismember him. Vaelor was considered by some amongst the Aether Guard, such as Kanan Jarrus, to have been the greatest warrior that the Order had during Synth Wars Chronicle, with the only possible exceptions being Zorath and Commander Tax. His former Student, Kestra Nil, once acknowledged Vaelor as someone powerful who rarely lost his battles.
Vaelor was particularly skilled in Form V —both the Shien and Djem So variants. He was able to deflect not only plasma rifle fire with ease, but also shots from freighters. He made good use of Djem So's strength-based strikes and counterattacks to overpower or overwhelm his opponent. Vaelor also used Jar'Kai on some occasions: He used Kenobi's aether blade during his first duel with Count Dooku on Geonosis, he wielded Barriss Offee's own aether blade against her along with his own, and he wielded Dooku's aether blade in addition to his own to execute him after defeating him on the Invisible Hand .
Vaelor was also adept in the use of Form IV , often incorporating its techniques (primarily acrobatics) into his dueling style. He was well aware that the key to deflecting the fire of multiple adversaries was fluidity of motion, which he mastered enough to having adjusted the classic techniques of Form IV to better defend against ranged attackers. He demonstrated this in a holographic recording, later found in Kanan Jarrus' holocron .
Darth Vader wields his Shadow Overlord aether blade.
After Darth Vader received his cybernetic enhancements and armor, his dueling style became visually more direct than it had been, presumably due to his armor and cybernetics affecting his physical abilities; however, Vader's style was still similar enough to how he fought as a Aether Guard that Admiral Tarkin was able to puzzle out Vader's former identity largely by observing him fight. Vader continued to use Jar'Kai on various occasions. On Infernus, he Aether pulled a Black Market Runner's aether blade and wielded it with his own to finish off the last remaining Synthetic Being in Lady Corvax's sanctum . Another time, Vader used his weapon with one of Darth Atrius's lightsabers against a group of thugs whom Sana Starros had hired to keep the Imperials off her back.
A pragmatic combatant both as Vaelor and later as Darth Vader, he had the skill to throw an activated aether blade to attack, guiding it with the Aether, or he would resort to lethally activating unsuspecting opponents' lightsabers. He once impressed Kenobi by telekinetically activating lightsabers (both in the arms dealer Chong's possession) and cutting him apart before pulling the lightsabers to his hands.
Vader absorbing plasma rifle bolt energy with his hand.
Blessed with impressive reflexes that were typical of a Aether Guard, Aether Guard Sage Sage Kormin saw enormous potential in the 9-year-old Vaelor and believed him to have been conceived by the Aether itself. Indeed, when his blood was tested, Vaelor's midi-chlorian count was off the scale—above 20,000, reportedly even greater than Zorath's. When he was nine, Anakin was already skilled in having quick reflexes and precognition. This was something he used to an advantage when he participated in pod racing. He was also able to sense what things were without looking at them. Vader was skilled at absorbing energy with his hands, using this ability to shield himself from Jax Korvan's plasma rifle fire on Cloud City and, more surprisingly, from a weapon loaded with a kyber crystal .
In his teen years, Vaelor's Aether training progressed to the point where he was able to manifest various abilities, and Kenobi thought of him as being "advanced for his age." He was very skilled at using the Aether to scan his surroundings, such as when he sensed poisonous kouhuns infiltrating Representative Lyra Vex's bedroom from standing outside. He was also skilled at sensing other people's feelings and disturbances in the Aether. Vaelor also showed aptitude for precognitive dreams, as when he foresaw the deaths of both his mother and his wife in dreams, but also of himself becoming a Aether Guard, back when he lived with his mother on Dustara. However, Vaelor struggled in using the Aether to control and befriend animals, noting that Kenobi was much better at it than he was.
Vaelor was skilled in telekinesis. Even as a late beginner, by his own admission, he was very good at it, better than any of his peers and most of the older Padawans as well. He could lift lightsabers in the air and ignite them with ease, as he did once to scare a pair of Padawans who had called him a "slave to his emotions." Despite having an exceptionally strong connection to the Aether, Vaelor tended not to use it very often during aether blade duels and focused instead on the physical aspects of combat. This tendency held even when facing opponents who made liberal use of the Aether during duels, such as Count Dooku. He also could make use of animal control.
During Synth Wars Chronicle, Anakin's strength and skill continued to increase. He became capable of utilizing telekinesis to accomplish such esoteric tasks as creating air bubbles around another person's head to enable breathing underwater, as shown when he saved Padmé's life at Mon Cala. During his mission to Utapau, Anakin was able to feel brain waves on a dead body, and when he first met Grand Admiral Thrawn, he was able to feel his thoughts by reaching out to him through the Aether. At one point during the war, Vaelor was able to telekinetically subdue both the Son and the Daughter simultaneously. However, this empowerment was lost when the Shadow Path grew too powerful and disrupted the balance in the Aether.
Vader uses the Aether to stop an AT-AT from crushing him on Cymoon 1.
After his cybernetic reconstruction, Vader's connection to the Aether strengthened considerably, to the point where his Sage Overlord Varakis once complimented his powers as being "unparalleled." Though his injuries had physically crippled him, Vader believed that the suffering that he had experienced had both perfected his spirit and freed him from the needs of the flesh, allowing him to concentrate solely on his relationship to the Aether. He demonstrated enough telekinetic strength to stop an AT-AT from stepping on him and had been in the process of tearing it apart before he was interrupted in doing so. On another occasion he telekinetically lifted two AT-DP walkers off of himself without any apparent strain. He also demonstrated the ability to grab hold of an engine-damaged Zelthar freighter and bring it to the ground, and crush a Lambda -class shuttle's engines in midflight to take it down. He was able to utilize a Aether push in a wave-like manner that was powerful enough to blow apart numerous lyleks simultaneously, despite the fact that the creatures' carapaces were impervious to even heavy plasma rifle fire.
Vader could also project Aether barriers to protect himself and others. These barriers were powerful enough to protect him while submerged in the lava of Infernus, though only for a short time. Vader was able to create a barrier strong enough to protect his son, Ryn Vaelor, from an attack by Sly Moore , who used two kyber crystal powered weapons at the same time. On at least one occasion, he demonstrated that he was able to telekinetically manipulate targets without the need to gesture at them as virtually all other Aether adepts did. This granted him the ability to throw objects with the Aether whilst actively engaged in swordplay. He was also capable of using the Aether Stasis ability and was skilled enough in its execution that on one occasion he used it to freeze two Inquisitors simultaneously.
Vader was also capable of using his telekinetic abilities to perform extremely delicate, complex, and precise tasks. On one occasion, he returned his badly damaged cybernetic limbs to perfect working order by dismantling a broken Synthetic Being and telekinetically repairing himself with its components. He also demonstrated the ability to crush internal organs with the Aether. At one point during an interrogation he used the Aether to inflict torturous amounts of pain on several insurgent prisoners, to the point of causing heart failure in one of them.
Although he already used the Aether choke as a Aether Guard, the use of this ability became very common for Vader, both in combat and for punishment.
Vaelor developed a tendency to use Aether choke over the course of his time as a Aether Guard, especially when angered or using it as a means of interrogation when an individual was uncooperative. Vaelor would go on to use this strength to deadly effect when he assumed the name Vader, generally using the choke to kill those who insulted him or failed to meet his expectations as well to instill dread. He also used the technique to twist, crush, or snap his victim's neck, notably executing Commander Fox this way as well as other Synth Soldiers and later Dominion Enforcers.
Like most Aether adepts, Vader also had the ability to vastly augment both his strength and speed with the Aether. He was also very skilled at using the Aether to leap great distances. During the mission to investigate Geonosis , Captain Rex remarked that Aether Guard Guardian Kanan Jarrus had yet to demonstrate Vaelor's skill with Aether jumps, despite having just jumped across a chasm more than 40 meters wide.
Vader demonstrated on numerous occasions the ability to probe the thoughts of others , including those of Aether adepts of similar strength to himself. Due to his strong connection with his son, Luke, he was able to sense his presence through the Aether quite easily—as opposed to his Sage, who was skeptical about this ability, and quickly became able to mentally communicate with him upon Luke learning he was Vader's son.
Even at a young age, Vaelor demonstrated a great aptitude for piloting, mechanics, and engineering. He rebuilt the protocol unit Protocol Synthetic Being X-7K, an advanced Synthetic Being fluent in over six million forms of communication, as well as a functional podracer. Although during his adulthood, he would rarely use his talent as a mechanic, he kept the skill of repairing technology. Vader had displayed great strength in hunting and exterminating the Aether Guard Order, leaving his later efforts against enemies like Crimson Dawn to require far less effort. Once most Aether Guard had been killed, Tarkin believed that Vader was actively searching for stimulating opponents to challenge himself.
Darth Vader's TIE Advanced
As a Flight Captain, Vaelor showed an amazing ability that few were able to surpass, and Kenobi would later describe him to his son Luke as "the best star Flight Captain in the galaxy." Anakin was able to Flight Captain a pod racer and was the first and only human thus far to compete in a pod race. He was also able to survive the Battle of Aetheris, and he went on to improve his skills in flying and aerial combat. Throughout Synth Wars Chronicle, Anakin was known for being the best Flight Captain in the United Systems Republic and was known for leading the charge in battles and coming out alive. He was very skilled in chasing down targets, shaking off-target locks, and outmaneuvering opponents. His strength in the Aether also gave him quick reflexes, an ability that would help in dodging fire and focusing in on enemy targets. He also displayed incredible precognition, as he was capable of predicting his targets' movements during dogfights with pinpoint precision. He once managed to land the Invisible Hand when it was half-destroyed and breaking up in the atmosphere.
After becoming Vader, he remained an exceptionally skilled Flight Captain, as shown when he single-handedly almost destroyed Phoenix Squadron in a lone TIE Advanced x1, easily out-flying their entire fleet and forcing their command to abandon ship. He also obliterated several Alliance squadrons during the Battle of Vrogas Vas. Despite this, Vaelor would occasionally be shot down during his combat piloting career, with Kestra Nil once proclaiming they always crashed when he flew, although Vaelor had a habit of blaming whatever ship it was he was flying. Although he mainly used lightsabers, Vaelor was extremely proficient at the use of plasma rifle pistols . He also had some training on the use of staves , a skill he once taught to a Felucian village so that they could defend themselves. Vaelor spoke Galactic Basic Standard , Huttese , Jawa Trade Language , and Meese Caulf , and he understood binary and the Bith language . As Vader, he learned the ancient Shadow Overlord tongue , and he spoke it along with his Sage, Sidious.
Vaelor's Student attire upon being taken as Sage Eldrin's Student.
As a slave on Dustara, Vaelor wore a tan tunic. Equipped with tools, he was able to rebuild Protocol Synthetic Being X-7K and complete his own podracer. During the Battle of Aetheris, he piloted a N-1 starfighter and wore a Aetheris Flight Captain's helmet. After becoming a part of the Aether Guard Order, he wore Aether Guard robes and was given a Student braid. He also kept a bit driver on himself, which he lost during the rescue of Zorath, and had his own aether blade that was destroyed on Geonosis. Afterward, he was given a replacement saber that had a green blade for the arena battle before having a new aether blade for Synth Wars Chronicle. During the early days of the war, Vaelor wore pieces of commander armor over his Aether Guard clothing. Later, he wore different Aether Guard robes without the armor, although he had donned the armor again when he met Commander Thrawn. Whilst undercover on the Halcyon , Vaelor wore a brown and grey outfit, which defined his muscular build, that included a cape.
